SUBJECT:
Developments in the Opium Monopoly
1-1088
of the Kwantung Leased Territory
THE HONORABLE
THE SECRETARY OF STATE
()
-1
WASHINGTON
C
SIR:
C
1 have the honor to report in regard to recent
developments in the opiun monopoly of the Kwantung
Leased Territory.
2
There are appended hereto translations made in
the Consulate of Imperial Ordinance No. 84 amending
A/286
895114
M
Imperial Ordinance No. 53* promulgated March z7, 1924,
as previously amended by Imperial Crdinances No. 167
of 1938, No. 380 of 1934 and No. 7 of 1937, and Kwan-
tung Bureau Ordinance No. 8 containing rules for the
enforcement of the above-mentioned Imperial Ordinance.
I am informed by the authorities of the Kwantung
Monopoly Bureau that these two Ordinances embody all
MAY 2 193E
laws directly concerned with opium, of the Kwantung
FILND
Leased Territory.
In brief, the Imperial Ordinance above-mentioned
provides for the manufacture by the Kwantung Monopoly
Bureau
translation of Imperial Ordinance No. 53 of 1924 was
appended to the Consulate's despatch to the Embassy at
Tokyo dated December 9, 1929.

=== Page 337 === (confidence 0.99)
一2-
Bureau of prepared opium as well as for the official
control of sales, hitherto exercised, and imposes
stricter measures of control over the possession of
opium. A relatively severe fine, Yen 3,000, and im-
prisonment for less than three years, are prescribed
for retail dealers who manufacture or adulterate pre-
pared opium. (This penalty, which is primarily de-
signed to protect the quality of the Monopoly's products,
contrasts strikingly with the mild punishment dealt
out to those convicted of the manufacture and sale of
other narcotics).
The Kwantung Bureau Ordinance contains detailed
regulations governing the control of permits issued to
addicts, and over the quantity of opiun obtainable by
these permits.
Supervisory rules concerning addicts
are set forth in detail, and retail dealers are re-
quired to keep records of their transactions and to
make reports of all sales to the police.
Shortly efter the promulgation of these Ordinances
I called on the newly appointed director of the Kwantung
(Opium) Monopoly Bureau, Mr. Noboru Yasunaga, who has
just been transferred fron his former post, now abolished,
of Civil Administrator in Port Arthur. He received me
very courteously, and was good enough to give me con-
siderable material for this report.
According to Mr. Yasunaga, the basic purpose of the
recent revisions is to make it possible for the authori-
ties to control more completely the manufacture and sale
of opium and to exercise closer supervision over addicts.
In

=== Page 338 === (confidence 0.99)
一3 -
In the past, he explained, the Monopoly Bureau has
acted simply as a "sales monopoly" which imported crude
opium and disposed of it to designated retailers. These
dealers were then free to manufacture prepared opium
from the crude opium and to dispose of it to addicts
having police permits to smoke.
The profit motive
being paramount in this system, the dealers were often
discovered to be adulterating the opium and engaging in
other dishonest practices. By the new system set up
under the amended laws, the Kwantung Monopoly Bureau
will act not merely as a sales monopoly but also as a
manufacturing monopoly.
The Bureau has recently
established a factory in Port Arthur which manufactures
prepared opium from the crude.
Dealers are now pro-
hibited from manufacturing or processing opium and act
simply as retail distributing agents for the Monopoly.
According to Mr. Yasunaga, the new laws will close a
number of loop-holes which formerly led to abuse, and
make it considerably easier for the government to ex-
crcise the control which, it hopes, will eventually
lead to the complete abolition of opium addiction in
this Territory.
Mr. Yasunaga is unquestionably correct in stating
that the new laws provide a greatly increased measure
of control over opium.
The steps taken would seem
definitely to be in the direction of the recommendations
of the Geneva Opium Convention to the effect that the
manufacture of opium should, as soon as possible, be
organized as a governmental enterprise, and would appear
to

=== Page 339 === (confidence 0.99)
一4-
to provide adequate machinery for effective control.
The question arises whether such control will be ex-
ercised in the direction of reducing the number of
addicts with a view to eventual abolition of the opium
evil, or whether, as in the past, the huge profits from
the sales of opium will prove to be an irresistible
temptation for the Government to use the Monopoly as
a source of revenue.
One of my consular colleagues
with whom I discussed the matter gsve it es his opinion
that the new system was "all eye-wash" and that it was
devised simply to divert more of the profits of the
traffic from the pockets of the retail dealers into
the government treasury.
The past records of monopoly systems in other por-
tions of the Japanese Empire would tend to bear out this
pessimistic view.
In Chosen and Taiwan the authorities
have never given evidence of any determired effort to
stamp out the opium evil and the number of addicts in
those territories shows little sign of diminishing.
Furthermore, the recent establishment in the Kwantung
Leased Territory of a factory for the production of pre-
pared opium and the housing of the office of the Bureau
in a handsome new edifice in Dairen would not seem to
Nor does
point to the early abolition of the traffic.
the appearance of the article sold by the Monopoly seem
designed to discourage sales. I enclose a sample of an
empty tube, looking like a small tooth paste container,
which is made by the Monopoly to contain its products.
The tube, and its covering carton, which were obtained
through

=== Page 340 === (confidence 0.98)
- 5 
through the kindness of Mr. Yasunaga, are attractively
designed and are labeled (in large characters) "Good-
1. Certainly
the appearance of the package is not calculated to im-
press the purchaser with the evil and destructive
nature of the contents.
On the other hand it seems very likely that wide-
spread criticism of the opium traffic in Japanese col-
onies, especially by American representatives in Geneva,
have had their effect and that the recent changes may
represent a real effort to exercise more effective con-
trol. Iu support of this view there is quoted an ex-
cerpt translated from the MANSHU NICHI NICI of February
22,1938.
"The need for revision (of the opium laws)
has been keenly felt especiaily in view of the
fact that in every meeting of the Opium Confer-
once in Geneve there is vociferous criticism of
the operation of government opium monopolies
and Japan has come in for considerable criticism.
It is believed that the amendments now introduced
into the laws will serve to quiet the critics...."
In considering the Kwantung opium monopoly, (which
incidentally is officially entitled simply the Kwantung
Monopoly without any indication as to the product which
it handles) it should be borne in mind that the activi-
ties of this organization are confined to the purchase
of crude opium, and the manufacture and sale of prepared
and medicinal opium. Its purchases of crude opium are
made chiefly from the Chosen Monopoly Bureau, and to a
lesser degree from Iran and Turkey through the agency of
Mitsui and other large firms. According to Mr. Yasunaga,
the Monopoly has no direct relationship or connection
with

=== Page 341 === (confidence 0.98)
- 6-
with similar monopolies in any other Japanese colony
or in Manchukuo" nor does it export opium in any form.
It handles opium only and does not deal in any other
form of narcotics. It has no control over smoking
permits, which are issued by the Kwantung Police, and
it has no authority to examine or supervise the addicts.
It is, in effect, simply an opiun manufacturing and whole-
sale selling organization operated by the Government.
The tube above-mentioned contains 5 grams of pre-
pared opium and is sold at retail for 90 sen. Cartons
containing 50 tubes are sold by the !onopoly to retail
dealers for Yen 35, allowing them a profit of Yen 10
per carton or about 30 per cent, which is an exceedingly
generous margin of profit on a monopoly product. Per-
haps in making the Monopoly product cheap and attractive
the authorities have in mind the competition from non-
monopoly opium. I learn from reliable sources that
"bootleg" opium can be purchased openly at restaurants,
hotels and even tobacco stands in this city, at prices
approximately the seme as those of the Monopoly's pro-
ducts.
I shall endeavor in a later despatch to discuss
other aspects of the Monopoly Bureau.
I have also
asked to be permitted to inspect the new opium factory
in Port Arthur, and hope to be able to make a report in
regard thereto in the near future. I have also asked
for information and statistics in regard to the govern-
ment's sanitarium for addicts, but have not thus far
been

=== Page 342 === (confidence 0.96)
一7-
been favored with a response.
Respectfully yours,
William T. Turner
American Consul

IMPERIAL ORDINANCE NO. 84
(Amending Imperial Ordinance No. 53 promulgated March 27,
19z4, as previously amended by Imperial Ordinances No.
167 of 1928, No. 380 of 1934 and No. 7 of 1937)
The Ordinance regulating opium in the Kwantung
Province is hereby amended as follows:
Article 2.
The word "smoke" is changed to "smoke pre-
pared opium sold under official auspices."
Article 3 (2).
The sale and dispensing of opium and
the manufacture of prepared opium and
medicinal opium shall be carried out by the authorities.
(2)
"When a retail dealer in prepared opium
shall manufacture smoking implements or shall
buy or sell, give, receive, own or possess in-
haling apparatus or prepared opium or opium."
Article 4 paragraph 2.
 The words "shall not buy or sell
(traffic)" are changed to "shall
not buy or sell, give, receive, own or possess (opium ash).
Nevertheless this clause shall not apply in case of an
addiot who, having obtained a permit in accordance with
the exemption set forth in Article 2, shall own or possess
opium ash which has been produced as the result of the
consumption of prepared opium."
Article 4 (new paragraph).
Retail dealers in prepared
opium may not process or
adulterate prepared opium.
Article 7.
The word "retail dealers in opium" appearing
in Section 1 shall be changed to "retail
dealers in prepared opium."
Article 9.

=== Page 344 === (confidence 0.98)
- 2 -
Artiole 9.
Paragraph 3 shall be changed to the follow-
ing: mThose who violate the provisions of
Article 4 (3) shall be imprisoned for less than three
years or shall be fined less than Yen 3,Ooo. Except
in cases of punishment under the provisions of the
first or second paragraphs of this Article, those who
violate the provisions of Article 4 and those who, being
addicts in receipt of permits under the exemption pro-
vided for in Article 2, consume opium other than that
prepared opium sold under official auspices, shall be
imprisoned for less than one year or fined less than
500 yen."
Article 15 (new).
The Ambassador may by order delegate
a portion of the authority conferred
under this law to the Governor of Kwantung Province.
Additional Rule. The date of enforcement of the present
law shall be fixed by the (Japanese)
Ambassador to Manchoukuo.

KWANTUNG BUREAU ORDINANCE NO. 8
Rules for the Enforoement of the
Kwantung Province Opium Law
article l.
a person desiring to obtain a permit for
the consumption of prepared opium shall make
application to the Governor of Kwantung Province, setting
forth his domicile, residence, name, age and occupation.
Article Z.
The Governor of Kwantung Province may, upon
receipt of the above-mentioned application,
grant such permit to a person who, being either a Man-
churian or Chinese of 2l years of age or over, has been
certified to be an opium addict by a designated physioian.
Article 3. Whenever a permit to consume prepared opium
is granted, an opium consumption certificate
shall be issued, in accordance with the appended form,
whereon shall be indicgted the daily dose and the opium
dealer from whom the addict shall procure his prepared
opium. Opium consumption certificates may not be
transferred and may not be loaned. The Governor of
wantung Province may cause the bearer of a amoking
certificate to be physically examined and may, at a
pecified time, or temporarily, alter the dosage or
withdraw the permit.
Artiole 4. In case of loss or destruction of a smoking
certificate, the certificate may be replaced
or reissued upon application being made to the Governor
of kwantung Province.
Article 5.
A person who has received a permit to amoke
opium may obtain prepared opium and a amoking
implement. A person who has received a permit to smoke
propared opium may not obtain prepared opium in exoess of
ten days supply of his fixed dosage. However, this rule
shall not apply in case be obtains a permit from the chief
of the oompetent police station for reason of travel or other un-
atbdrable rèasohe A smoker may not be in possession of a larger
quantity of opium than that specified in the foregoing paragraph.
artiele 6. a person who has received a permit to amoke
prepared opium may not obtain prepared opium
or smoking implements without exhibiting his smoking cer-
tificate.
Article 7.

Artiole 7. Opium ash and prepared opium containers
which remain from the consumption of pre-
pared opium shall be disposed of in accordance with the
directions of police officials exoept when such articles
are bought in by the authorities.
Article 8. A person who has received permission to con-
sume prepared opium may not procure prepared
opium from any person other than hs designated retail pre-
pared opium dealer, and he may not receive a amoking im-
plement exoept from a retail prepared opium dealer. In
oase a person who has received pe rmission to consume pre-
pared opium desires to change his designated retail dealer
in prepared opium, he may make application to that effect
of the Governor of Kwantung Province.
Article 9. In case a person who has received permission
to consume prepared opium shall change his
domioile, residence or name or in case of his death, such
matter shall within ten days be reported either by the
person himself or by his relatives to the Governor of
kwantung Province.
Article 10. The Kwantung Monopoly Bureau shall sell and
distribute opium and shall manufacture pre-
pared opium and medicinal opium.
Article ll. In case dealers in medicinal opium, retail
prepared opium dealers, and manufacturing
chemists having permission to purchase opium require
opium they shall make application therefor to the Kwan-
tung Monopoly Bureau.
Article l2. When sohools and laboratories require opium
for purposes of experimentation they may
make application therefor to the Kwantung Monopoly Bureau
upon receipt of a certificate from the competent ohief of
police. The opium mentioned in the foregoing provision
may be procured free of charge.
Article 13. Dealers in medicinal opium shall be selected
from among pharmmcists and drug dealers by the
Governor of kwantung Province. When physicians, dentists,
veterinary surgeons, and pharmacists require medical opium
they may apply for it from the dealers desoribed in the
foregoing olause upon receipt of a certificate from the
competent ohief of police. However, when less than 25
grams of medicinal opium is required for compounding pur-
poses within one year, such certificate shall not be re-
quired.
Artiole l4. Except in case such as those mentioned in the
foregoing article, medicinal opium may not be
obtained or dispensed without a presoription-slip by a
physician, dentist, or veterinary surgeon.
Artiole 15.

shall be made to the Governor of wantung Province within

Article 17.
Retail dealers in prepared opium shall be

article 18. A person who is the beneficiary of the de-

may sell prepared opium and may manufacture and sell opium

rticle 19. When a retail dealer in prepared opium de-

article 20. Retail dealers in prepared opium shall not
procure prepared opium from any source other
than the Kwantung Monopoly Bureau. However, this pro-
vision shall not apply in regard to the disposition under

in cases wherein permission has been received from the

Article 2l. Retail dealers in prepared opium shall not
dispose of opium smoking implements except
in cases where a smoking certificate has been exhibited by
the person who has received permission to consume prepared
opium.
article 22. Retail dealers in prepared opium shall not
dispense prepared opium to any person other
than an opium smoker.in respect of whom he has been designated.
Artiole 23. A retail dealer in prepared opium shall not
dispense prepared opium in excess of the
limits imposed under Article 5.
Artiole z4. Dealers in medicinal opium and dealers in
prepared opium shall keep records of opium
and opium amoking implements purchased and sold. The
record of prepared opium and opium smoking implements
purehased shall indicate clearly each time a purchase

competent chief of police by the 5th of each month in re-
gard to receipts and disbursals of medicinal opium, pre-
pared opium and opium smoking implements.during the previous
month.
Article c6. When it is desired to dispose of opium and
opium smoking implements in the following
instances, a report in regard to the facts of the matter
shall be made, within ten days of the occurrence thereof,
to the Governor of Kwantung Province and his permission
obtained. In case of the death of the person concerned,
the procedure shall be carried out by his successor or by
relatives.
1.
When a dealer in medicinal opium or a

facturing chemist shall have had his designa-
tion or his permit cancelled or shall abandon
his business or shall die.
2.
When a physician, or dentist, or veterinary

3.
When a person who has received a permit to

Retail dealers in prepared opium and dealers

Article 28. In case of violation of the Kwantung Province

orders based on the present ordinance, or in case of dis-
honest conduct of business, on the part of a dealer in

3, Artiole 6, Article 8 clause 1, Article 14, Article 18

Article 32.
When the representative, family head, re
latives, cohabitor, employee or other per-

Article 33. The panal clausesof the present ordinance
shall, in case they are found applicable to

applicable to a minor person or to a legally incompetent
person, they shall be applied to the legal representative.
However, this provision shall not apply to a minor person
who in the conduct of his business has the same mental
capacity as an adult.
additional Rules.

SUBJECT:
Kwantung Opium Monoply
THE HONORABLE
THE SECRETARY OF STATE.
WASHINGTON.
SIR:
I have the honor to transmit a copy in free
translation of an interesting iten fron a local
1.1
Japanese newspaper i. regard to the opium monoply
O
of the Kwantung Leased Territory.
It will be recalled that there were promulguted
early this year ordinances containinc elaborate pro-
visions for the stricter control over opium in this
Territory*.
At that tire the authorities declared
that the new legislation would close the loopholes
JAN 13 1939
FILRD
which formerly led to abuse and would make it con-
siderably easier for the government to exercise the
control which, it was hoped, would eventually lead
to the complete abolition of opium addiction in this
Territory.
Judging by the appended newspaper report, the
hopes
114
(24)
*Vide Consulate's despatohos/No. 9 of March 4, 1938 and
No. 26/of June 6, 1938 in/ regard to the opium monoply.
118

=== Page 359 === (confidence 0.99)
-2-
hopes of the authorities have not only been nullified:
there would seem to ive lee  positive inerease in
addiction among the residents of the city particularly
the Japanese. If, as stated in the report, there are
1,ooo Japanese addicts who are registered as such by
the police there nust be many ore who are not so regis-
tered.
Probably only those persons are registered whose
addiction to the habit as brought them to the attention
of the authorities.
Certainly the desire for regular
doses of opium does not necessitate registration, as
non-monoply opium can be purchased freely and openly
in this city.
I have myself purchased non-monoply opium
in the restaurant of the Ryoto liotel, Dairen's second
largest hostelry owned partially by the South Manchuria
Railway, and in the largest and most respectable Chinese
restaurant in the city.
Both of these restaurants
provide smoking couches and paraphernalia for the use
of their guests and make no attempt at concealment.
The fact that the number of Japanese addicts is
showing an increase, deplorable as it is, may contain
a certain promise of hope for future improvement.
As long as the victims of the traffic were only Chinese
and Koreans, the authorities were inclined to be com-
placent. But a marked increase in opium addiction
among the Japanese themselves, who have hitherto been
accounted largely free from the habit, will arouse the
authorities to action as nothing else would.
The reluctance of the authorities to take positive
steps

=== Page 360 === (confidence 0.99)
- 3 -
steps to stamp out the opium evil may eusily be sur-
mised from the budget accounts of the Kwantung Loased
Territory. Out of a total revenue estimated for the
current fiscal year at Yen 23 millions, the revenue from
Monoply Bureau was estir ated at Yen 2,216,970: truly
an enormous profit from the sale of opium in a territory
with a population of only 1,200,000!
Respectfully yours,
Williar. T. Turner
American Consul

=== Page 361 === (confidence 0.98)
Enolosure to despatoh No. 60 dated November 29, 1938, from
William T. Turner, American Consul at Dairen, Manchuria,
entitled "Kwantung Opium Monoply"
OURCJ:
MAS HICAI.ICHI
November 27, 1938.
Free Translation
In the effort to abolish completely addiction to
opium smoking the authorities of the Kwantung Sanitarium
for addicts have since last yeur followed the example set
by Manchukuo and have carried out compulsory cure of Jap-
anese, Koreans and Chinese. At the same time steps were
taken by the Opium Monoply to enforce compliance with
stricter regulations upon these dishonest dealers who had
been evading the law. However, opium addiction n d dis-
honest dealers are still flourishing, and with the approach
of cold weather there has been noted an inerease in the
number of persons found lying unconcious and dead from the
drug. Accordingly the sanitarium authorities have deter-
mined upon further efforts to att ck and overcome the evil
of opium addiction.
As a first step the authorities dosi to augument
their slender personnel, but this they f'd difric it
for budgetary reasons. Consequently they Plan to take
immediate curative measures in respect of Japanese ad-
dicts. In Dairen city alone there are more than 1,000
Japanese men and women addicts, anong which are servants,
geishas, wives, clerks, salari ed-men, business men in
fact people from all classes. The cure of these people
is a problem of no small proportions. It has been de-
cided to draw up a "black list" of such addicts and to
proceed with their cure as they come to hand.
Ir. Sakui, Chief of the Sanitarium, has declared
as follows:
"Thanks to the efforts of the Police since
last year a large number of addicts have been
treated and cured. Nowever recently the number
of addicts has again shown an increasing tendency.
Especially notable is the fact that in Dairen a-
lone the number of Japanese addicts has increased
to over loo0, wh ose broken bodies seen stretched
out in the back alleys are a reproach to our coun-
try in these times of stress. Much has been done
through the cooperation of the authorities, but
however successful a cure may be in the case of
employed persons, the cure of unemployed persons
does not remain permanent and such persons promptly
revert to their evil ways".
